### [91] [Characteristic Mode Analysis of Composite Nanostructures using a Coupled System of Volume Integral and Hydrodynamic Equations](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.29709)
*Meruyert Khamitova,Ran Zhao,Doolos Aibek Uulu,Sebastian Celis Sierra,Hakan Bagci*

Main category: physics.comp-ph

TL;DR: This paper develops full-structure and sub-structure characteristic mode analysis (CMA) formulations for composite metallic-dielectric nanostructures based on coupled volume integral equations (VIE) and hydrodynamic equations (HDE). The full-structure CMA builds a generalized eigenvalue equation from the complete coupled system, while the sub-structure CMA eliminates dielectric-region unknowns to focus on metallic-region currents, improving computational efficiency while preserving accuracy when the dielectric does not resonate. Both are validated against extinction cross-section results and used to study substrate-induced spectral shifts and hybridization.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To enable efficient and physically interpretable modal analysis of composite metallic-dielectric nanostructures, which is important for understanding how dielectric environments reshape plasmonic resonances (e.g., substrate-induced red-shifts and hybridization) without relying solely on full-wave extinction simulations.

Method: The authors formulate CMA within a coupled VIE-HDE framework. Full-structure CMA constructs the generalized eigenvalue equation from the complete coupled system matrix. Sub-structure CMA derives a reduced system by eliminating dielectric-region unknowns via Schur complement-like reduction, yielding a smaller eigenvalue problem in terms of metallic currents only. Both formulations are validated against extinction cross-section (ECS) results.

Result: Both CMA formulations reproduce the extinction cross-section results. The sub-structure CMA is computationally more efficient due to the reduced matrix dimension and identifies the same resonances as the full-structure CMA when the dielectric is non-resonant in the band of interest. The analysis characterizes substrate-induced red-shifts and hybridized modal responses for high-contrast substrates in direct contact.

Conclusion: The proposed full- and sub-structure CMA formulations provide robust and efficient tools for analyzing composite metallic-dielectric nanostructures, enabling physical insight into how dielectric environments alter metallic resonances. The sub-structure approach offers computational advantages without loss of resonance identification accuracy in non-resonant dielectric scenarios.

Abstract: Full-structure and sub-structure characteristic mode analysis (CMA) formulations are developed for composite metallic--dielectric nanostructures based on a coupled system of volume integral equations (VIE) and the hydrodynamic equation (HDE). In the full-structure CMA, the generalized eigenvalue equation (GEE) is constructed from the matrix of the complete coupled system, and the resulting characteristic currents describe the response of the entire composite nanostructure. In the sub-structure CMA, the GEE is constructed from a reduced system, derived from the coupled system by eliminating the dielectric-region unknowns, so that it is expressed only in terms of the metallic-region currents. This isolates the resonances of the metallic region while still incorporating the effect of the dielectric region through the reduced system. Because the reduced system has a smaller dimension, the sub-structure CMA is computationally more efficient than the full-structure CMA and, when the dielectric does not resonate in the frequency range of interest, identifies the same resonances. Both formulations are validated against extinction cross-section (ECS) results and are used to characterize how a dielectric environment reshapes the metallic resonances, including substrate-induced red-shifts and, for high-contrast substrates in direct contact, hybridized modal responses.

</details>


### [92] [A wide-range temperature-dependent deep potential for sodium with near-experimental accuracy from melting to the critical point](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.29826)
*G. S. Demyanov,D. V. Minakov,V. B. Fokin,P. R. Levashov*

Main category: physics.comp-ph

TL;DR: The paper develops temperature-dependent deep potentials for sodium using finite-temperature DFT data with PBE, AM05, and r²SCAN functionals, and investigates thermophysical properties from room temperature to the critical region. The r²SCAN-based model accurately reproduces critical parameters, melting temperature, transport properties, and thermodynamic consistency.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand the thermophysical properties of sodium across a wide temperature range—including the critical region—where experimental data are sparse, and to assess the influence of the exchange-correlation functional on predicted properties, particularly critical parameters.

Method: Construction of temperature-dependent deep potentials for sodium trained on finite-temperature DFT data using PBE, AM05, and r²SCAN functionals; followed by molecular dynamics simulations including direct solid-liquid and liquid-vapor coexistence simulations, as well as direct acoustic simulations for speed of sound validation.

Result: The r²SCAN-based model gives critical temperature Tc=2.508(8) kK, density ρc=0.203(4) g/cm³, and pressure Pc=0.249(6) kbar, in close agreement with recommended values. Normal-pressure bcc density differs by only 0.06% from experiment. Melting temperature is 346(2) K (25 K below recommended). Surface tension approaches zero near the critical point, and transport properties extend into expanded-liquid and near-critical regions.

Conclusion: The r²SCAN-based deep potential provides accurate, thermodynamically consistent predictions of sodium's thermophysical properties from ambient to critical conditions. The results confirm the importance of functional choice and demonstrate the reliability of deep potentials for modeling liquid metals near the critical point.

Abstract: We construct temperature-dependent deep potentials for sodium using finite-temperature DFT data obtained with the PBE, AM05, and $r^2$SCAN exchange-correlation functionals and investigate the thermophysical properties of sodium from room temperature to the critical region. The predicted critical parameters show a dependence on the exchange-correlation functional. The model based on the $r^2$SCAN functional gives a critical temperature $T_c=2.508(8)$ kK, density $ρ_c=0.203(4)$ g/cm$^{3}$, and pressure $P_c=0.249(6)$ kbar, in close agreement with the recommended values. This model is then used to reconstruct the normal-pressure and critical isobars and to calculate the enthalpy, heat capacities, thermal expansion coefficient, bulk moduli, Grüneisen parameter, and speed of sound. The calculated normal-pressure bcc density differs by only 0.06% from the experimental value. Direct solid-liquid coexistence simulations give a melting temperature of $346(2)$ K, 25 K below the recommended value, consistent with the sensitivity expected from meV/atom free-energy errors. Liquid-vapor coexistence simulations reproduce the binodal and yield a surface tension that approaches zero near the critical point. The calculated self-diffusion coefficient and shear viscosity extend the available transport-property data into the expanded-liquid and near-critical regions, where direct experimental information is sparse. We also demonstrate the thermodynamic consistency of the results by comparing the speed of sound obtained from direct acoustic simulations with that calculated from the equation of state.

</details>


### [93] [Cartesian tensor equivariant machine-learning force field for spin-dependent atomistic simulations](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.30338)
*Junjie Wang,Yijie Zhu,Zhongwei Zhang,Zhiyue Guo,Xudong Zhu,Lixin He,Chi Ding,Jian Sun*

Main category: physics.comp-ph

TL;DR: HotPP-Spin is a spin-dependent extension of the HotPP machine learning interatomic potential, built on Cartesian tensor equivariant message passing, that treats atomic magnetic moments as explicit axial-vector degrees of freedom to unify exchange-dominated and spin-orbit-induced magnetic interactions. It accurately captures collinear magnetism, noncollinear magnetism, and magnetic anisotropy, and reproduces the finite-size magnetic ordering crossover temperature in H-phase monolayer VSe2 in close agreement with experiment.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Magnetic materials exhibit complex coupling between atomic structure and spin degrees of freedom, which poses a fundamental challenge for atomistic simulations across experimentally relevant length and time scales. Existing approaches lack a unified, general framework for representing both exchange-dominated and spin-orbit-induced magnetic interactions without imposing predefined analytical interaction forms.

Method: HotPP-Spin builds on Cartesian tensor equivariant message passing. Atomic magnetic moments are treated as explicit axial-vector degrees of freedom, with spatial-inversion and time-reversal parities propagated through tensor couplings. A scalar spin-dependent potential energy surface yields energy-conserving atomic forces and magnetic effective fields via differentiation.

Result: Benchmarks across collinear magnetism, noncollinear magnetism, and spin-orbit-coupling-induced magnetic anisotropy show accurate description of magnetic energy landscapes, magnetic forces, and magnetic-order-dependent energy-volume relations. For H-phase monolayer VSe2, stochastic spin-dynamics simulations locate the finite-size magnetic ordering crossover at 415-435 K, closely matching the experimental value of 418.5±7.8 K.

Conclusion: Cartesian tensor message passing is established as a general route for connecting first-principles magnetic energetics with large-scale atomistic simulations of coupled structural and spin phenomena, enabling accurate modeling without recourse to predefined interaction forms.

Abstract: Magnetic materials exhibit an intricate coupling between atomic structure and spin degrees of freedom, posing a fundamental challenge for atomistic simulations across experimentally relevant length and time scales. Here we introduce HotPP-Spin, a spin-dependent extension of HotPP for magnetic machine learning interatomic potentials, built on Cartesian tensor equivariant message passing. Atomic magnetic moments are treated as explicit axial-vector degrees of freedom, while spatial-inversion and time-reversal parities are propagated through the tensor couplings. This construction provides a unified representation of exchange-dominated and spin-orbit-induced interactions without imposing predefined analytical interaction forms. A scalar spin-dependent potential energy surface yields energy-conserving atomic forces and magnetic effective fields through differentiation. Benchmarks spanning collinear magnetism, noncollinear magnetism, and spin-orbit-coupling-induced magnetic anisotropy show that HotPP-Spin accurately describes magnetic energy landscapes, magnetic forces, and magnetic-order-dependent energy-volume relations within the same general framework. For H-phase monolayer VSe\(_2\), stochastic spin-dynamics simulations using the learned magnetic effective fields locate the finite-size magnetic ordering crossover at 415--435~K, in close numerical agreement with the reported experimental value of \(418.5\pm7.8\)~K. These results establish Cartesian tensor message passing as a general route for connecting first-principles magnetic energetics with large-scale atomistic simulations of coupled structural and spin phenomena.

</details>


### [94] [OpenMUSTANC (MUltiple Scattering Theory At Nanoplasmonic Cavities): A MATLAB toolbox for the simulation of Plasmonic Sphere Aggregates](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.30565)
*Xin Zheng,Christos Mystilidis,Christos Tserkezis,Guy A. E. Vandenbosch,Xuezhi Zheng*

Main category: physics.comp-ph

TL;DR: This paper presents a MATLAB toolbox for computational mesoscopic electromagnetics (CMEM) that simulates multiple spherical interfaces with arbitrary relative positions, incorporating mesoscopic physical models (HDM, GNOR, SRM) to study nonlocal effects in nanometric structures.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: The combination of classical electromagnetism with mesoscopic material models (HDM, GNOR, SRM) calls for new computational electromagnetic algorithms or updates of conventional ones. There is a need for a general and efficient computational toolbox to investigate nonlocal effects in nanometric structures.

Method: The toolbox uses vector spherical wave functions to express electric and magnetic fields, an S matrix formulation for efficient treatment of incident and scattered fields at spherical interfaces, and a translation matrix for handling propagating waves with different expansion centers. Excitation sources include arbitrarily polarized plane waves, dipoles, and electron beams. Post-processing includes cross sections, far/near-field mapping, fluorescence enhancement, Purcell factor, quantum yield, cathodoluminescence, and electron energy-loss probability.

Result: A modular, highly efficient and general MATLAB toolbox for CMEM simulations of multiple spherical interfaces is developed, enabling the study of nonlocal effects with HDM, GNOR, and SRM models under various excitation sources.

Conclusion: The paper provides a full explanation and detailed guidelines for the proposed toolbox, making it a valuable resource for researchers in the nanoplasmonics community.

Abstract: Mesoscopic physical models, including the Hydrodynamic Drude Model (HDM), the Generalized Nonlocal Optical Response (GNOR) Model, and the Surface Response Model (SRM), have been proposed to investigate nonlocal effects in nanometric structures. The combination of classical electromagnetism with these mesoscopic material models calls for new computational electromagnetic (CEM) algorithms, or update of conventional ones, in what is termed computational mesoscopic electromagnetics (CMEM). In this work, we present a MATLAB toolbox for the simulation of multiple spherical interfaces with arbitrary relative positions, with the incorporation of the aforementioned mesoscopic models. The method exploits vector spherical wave functions to properly express electric and magnetic fields, an S matrix formulation for the efficient treatment of incident and scattered fields at spherical interfaces, and a translation matrix to deal with propagating waves with different expansion centers. Excitation sources can be chosen among arbitrarily polarized plane waves, dipoles and electron beams. For the post-processing part, the calculation of cross sections and far/near-field mapping; fluorescence enhancement, Purcell factor and quantum yield; and cathodoluminescence and electron energy-loss probability is implemented. The toolbox is built in a modular manner, and each part (routine) has its own important functionality. This paper provides a full explanation of the proposed highly efficient and general toolbox, and a detailed guideline for researchers in the nanoplasmonics community.

</details>

### [95] [Advancing Lead-Free FASnI3 Perovskite Solar Cells via Cu-Doped NiOx HTL Engineering: An Optoelectronic Analysis](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.29341)
*Md. Faiaad Rahman,Jabed Hasan,Md. Ashaduzzaman Niloy,Ahmed Zubair*

Main category: physics.app-ph

TL;DR: This paper demonstrates that Cu-doped NiOx as a hole transport layer in lead-free FASnI3 perovskite solar cells, optimized via coupled FDTD optical and FEM electrical simulations, achieves a peak power conversion efficiency of 26.06% (with ARC), attributed to improved hole extraction and suppressed recombination losses.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Lead-free perovskite solar cells, particularly FASnI3-based devices, suffer from inefficient charge transport and significant recombination losses, limiting their efficiency compared to lead-based counterparts. A high-performance hole transport layer with favorable optoelectronic properties is needed to improve charge extraction and reduce carrier losses.

Method: The authors employed a coupled numerical framework combining finite-difference time-domain (FDTD) optical simulations with finite element method (FEM)-based electrical modeling. Electrical analysis solves the Poisson equation, drift-diffusion equations, and carrier continuity equations while accounting for radiative, non-radiative, and interfacial recombination. Systematic optimization of layer thicknesses and doping concentrations was performed, followed by integration of an anti-reflection coating (ARC).

Result: Optimized device achieved a PCE of 24.71%. Adding an ARC suppressed reflection losses, yielding a 5.47% relative enhancement, leading to a peak PCE of 26.06% with Jsc = 28.98 mA/cm2, Voc = 1.051 V, and FF = 85.64%.

Conclusion: Cu-doped NiOx serves as a highly promising HTL for lead-free FASnI3 perovskite solar cells, effectively boosting charge collection and maximizing overall device efficiency by improving hole extraction and suppressing recombination losses.

Abstract: Efficient lead-free perovskite solar cells remain limited by inefficient charge transport and significant recombination losses. In this work, copper (Cu) doped nickel oxide (NiOx) is employed as the hole transport layer (HTL) in a formamidinium tin iodide (FASnI3) perovskite solar cell to improve hole extraction and suppress carrier losses. The optoelectronic response of the proposed device architecture is evaluated through a coupled numerical framework combining finite-difference time-domain (FDTD) optical simulations with finite element method (FEM)-based electrical modeling. Electrical analysis solves the Poisson equation together with the drift-diffusion and carrier continuity equations, while accounting for radiative, non-adiative, and interfacial recombination mechanisms. Systematic optimization of the thicknesses and doping concentrations of the constituent layers was performed to establish a high-performance device configuration. Following optimization of layer dimensions and carrier densities, the device achieved a power conversion efficiency (PCE) of 24.71%. Integrating an anti-reflection coating (ARC) effectively suppressed reflection losses, yielding a 5.47% relative enhancement in PCE. Consequently, the optimized device achieved a peak PCE of 26.06%, with a short-circuit current density (Jsc) of 28.98 mA/cm2, an open-circuit voltage (Voc) of 1.051 V, and a fill factor (FF) of 85.64%. These findings demonstrate that Cu-doped NiOx functions as a highly promising HTL to boost charge collection and maximize overall efficiency in lead-free FASnI3 solar cells.

</details>


### [96] [Arctic Dispersion Interruption Phenomenon and Sound Source Depth Estimation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.30360)
*Weng Jinbao,Yang Yanming,Chen Benqing,Xu Dewei,Zhou Hongtao*

Main category: physics.app-ph

TL;DR: The paper investigates a normal mode waveguide in the deep Arctic Ocean's surface layer, where modal amplitude zeros occur when the source or receiver is near an eigenfunction node, causing dispersion interruption in time-frequency analysis. This enables source depth estimation using the interruption frequency and the node depth-frequency relationship from the sound speed profile, validated by simulations and experiments.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To exploit the dispersion interruption phenomenon in the Arctic Ocean's normal mode waveguide as a means of estimating source depth, which is otherwise challenging in such environments.

Method: The method involves identifying the interruption frequency from time-frequency analysis of received signals, then combining it with the precomputed node depth versus modal frequency relationship derived from the ocean sound speed profile to estimate the source depth given a known receiver depth.

Result: The dispersion interruption phenomenon and the source depth estimation method were successfully validated through both simulations and experiments.

Conclusion: The study demonstrates that dispersion interruption—caused by modal nulls at eigenfunction nodes—can serve as a reliable basis for estimating source depth in the deep Arctic Ocean's surface waveguide.

Abstract: The sound speed profile in the deep Arctic Ocean causes the surface layer to form a normal mode waveguide. When the depth of the sound source or receiver is near a node of the eigenfunction, the source cannot excite the mode, or the receiver cannot detect it, resulting in the modal amplitude at the receiver being approximately zero. This manifests as a dispersion interruption in the dispersion structure, which can be observed through time-frequency analysis of the received acoustic signal. Based on the interruption frequency identified from the received signal, combined with the relationship between node depth and modal frequency calculated from the ocean sound speed profile, the source depth can be estimated if the receiver depth is known. The phenomenon of dispersion interruption and the method of estimating source depth have been validated through simulations and experiments.

</details>


### [97] [Towards an effective medium theory for in-vivo Magnetic Resonance: Characterizing Admissible Magnetization Dynamics](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.30401)
*Alessandro Sbrizzi,Miha Fuderer,Ray Sheombarsing*

Main category: physics.app-ph

TL;DR: The paper proposes an effective-medium framework for magnetic resonance dynamics, replacing the classical linear relaxation term in the Bloch equation with a general nonlinear field constrained by physical principles (rotational symmetry, global stability of thermal equilibrium, dissipativity). This yields a complete characterization of admissible relaxation fields and recovers classical Bloch dynamics as a special case.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: The classical Bloch equation may be insufficient to capture complex effective dynamics in heterogeneous materials such as biological tissue, motivating the need for a more general macroscopic description of magnetic resonance dynamics.

Method: An effective-medium strategy is employed: the classical Larmor torque is retained while the relaxation term is replaced by a general nonlinear field (the effective medium term), constrained by key physical properties including rotational symmetry, global stability of thermal equilibrium, and dissipativity.

Result: The imposed physical constraints lead to a complete characterization of admissible relaxation fields and a constructive framework for generating nonlinear relaxation models. The classical Bloch dynamics is recovered as a special case of the proposed theory.

Conclusion: The theory provides a principled foundation for effective-medium descriptions of magnetic resonance phenomena at macro-scale, addressing limitations of the classical Bloch equation in heterogeneous media.

Abstract: The classical Bloch equation forms the foundation of magnetic resonance theory but may be insufficient to capture the complex effective dynamics observed in heterogeneous materials such as biological tissue. Here an effective-medium strategy is proposed to describe magnetic resonance dynamics from observations at macro-scale (order 1 mm$^3$). In this framework, the classical Larmor torque is retained while the relaxation term is replaced by a general nonlinear field (i.e. the effective medium term). This field is required to preserve key physical properties, including rotational symmetry, global stability of thermal equilibrium, and dissipativity. These requirements lead to a complete characterization of admissible relaxation fields and provide a constructive framework for generating nonlinear relaxation models. The resulting theory recovers classical Bloch dynamics as a special case and provides a principled foundation for effective-medium descriptions of magnetic resonance phenomena.

</details>


### [98] [A model-inversion control strategy to attain desired equilibrium points when synthesizing nonlinear resonators](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.30626)
*Maxime Morell,Emanuele De Bono,Emmanuel Gourdon,Manuel Collet,Alireza Ture Savadkoohi,Claude-Henri Lamarque*

Main category: physics.app-ph

TL;DR: The paper proposes a control strategy that attains desired equilibrium points in Electroacoustic Resonators (EARs) with nonlinear synthetic impedance by defining an exosystem with a desired nonlinear impedance and synthetic excitation, integrated in real time and enforced via model-inversion feedforward. Validated numerically and experimentally in quasi-open and enclosed acoustic environments.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: The initial conditions of a deterministic nonlinear system determine the asymptotic solutions reached, which is problematic when nonlinear behavior is achieved via active control and initial conditions cannot be pre-constrained. For Electroacoustic Resonators employing generalized impedance control, the actually manifested equilibrium points are not favorable for noise attenuation under realistic excitations since initial conditions are hardly accessible.

Method: Define an exosystem characterized by a desired nonlinear impedance and a synthetic external excitation whose dynamics are integrated in real time. Enforce the exosystem response onto the actual Electroacoustic Resonator via a model-inversion feedforward control strategy.

Result: The proposed algorithm is implemented in the Electroacoustic Resonator and validated both numerically and experimentally in a quasi-open acoustic environment and in enclosed cavities for acoustic mode attenuation.

Conclusion: The proposed exosystem-based control strategy successfully attains the desired equilibrium points in the synthetic nonlinear dynamics of Electroacoustic Resonators, enabling effective noise attenuation in cavities despite inaccessible initial conditions.

Abstract: The initial conditions of a deterministic nonlinear system affects the attained asymptotic solutions. This is a problem when the nonlinear behaviour is achieved by active control synthesis, and the initial conditions are not accessible for pre-constraining. Meanwhile, the generalized impedance control is recently gaining increasing attention in acoustics, and has been enlarged to achieve nonlinear mechanical responses of Electroacoustic Resonators at low excitation levels. Nevertheless, as the initial conditions of Electroacoustic Resonators are hardly accessible, the equilibrium points actually showcased by the synthetic nonlinear dynamics are not advantageous for noise attenuation in cavities excited by realistic noise sources. In this paper, we propose a control strategy being able to attain the desired equilibrium points, due to the definition of an exosystem characterized by a desired nonlinear impedance and a synthetic external excitation. The exosystem dynamics is integrated in real time and its response is enforced in the actual system (in our case, the Electroacoustic Resonator), by a model-inversion feedforward strategy. The proposed algorithm is implemented in the Electroacoustic Resonator, and is validated both numerically and experimentally. In particular, the experimental testing is conducted both in a quasi-open acoustic environment, and in enclosed cavities for acoustic mode attenuation.

</details>


### [99] [Bounds on Shaping Partially Coherent Microwaves with Programmable Scattering Systems](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.30729)
*Philipp del Hougne*

Main category: physics.app-ph

TL;DR: This paper derives fundamental performance bounds for manipulating partially coherent microwaves using programmable scattering systems (e.g., reconfigurable intelligent surfaces). It considers two problems—concentrating partially coherent power into a single output port and general coherency-matrix synthesis—and develops prototype-aware bounds by combining multiport network theory with semidefinite relaxation. The bounds are evaluated on four experimental RIS-parametrized MIMO systems with up to 100 1-bit elements, showing tightness compared to feasible discrete-optimization outcomes, while revealing that simpler bounds can sometimes outperform fully prototype-aware ones for coherency synthesis.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To provide certified, architecture-independent limits on what programmable scattering systems can achieve when manipulating partially coherent microwave fields, accounting for real-world constraints such as mutual coupling, loss, discrete tunability, and static scattering—an area lacking rigorous performance bounds.

Method: The authors combine multiport network theory (MNT) with semidefinite relaxation (SDR) to derive several classes of bounds: (1) prototype-aware bounds for single-output power concentration from partially coherent inputs, (2) architecture-independent bounds on fidelity and useful strength for general coherency-matrix synthesis, (3) prototype-specific scalar refinements, and (4) fully prototype-aware SDR bounds on the useful-strength–fidelity Pareto frontier. These are validated using proxy-MNT models estimated from measurements of four experimental RIS-parametrized MIMO systems with up to 100 1-bit programmable elements.

Result: The derived bounds are generally tight when compared against feasible discrete-optimization outcomes across the four measured RIS-parametrized MIMO systems. Notably, for coherency synthesis, simpler bounds can sometimes be tighter than the fully prototype-aware bounds, demonstrating their complementarity and practical value.

Conclusion: The work provides certified performance limits for wave-domain processing and harvesting of partially coherent microwaves with programmable scattering systems, offering both architecture-independent and prototype-aware tools that account for realistic impairments, and highlighting the complementary roles of simple and detailed bound formulations.

Abstract: We derive bounds on manipulating partially coherent microwaves with programmable scattering systems. We first consider the concentration of power from a partially coherent input into a single output port and derive a prototype-aware bound by combining multiport network theory (MNT) with semidefinite relaxation (SDR). We then address general coherency-matrix synthesis, deriving architecture-independent bounds on fidelity and useful strength, prototype-specific scalar refinements thereof, and fully prototype-aware SDR bounds on the useful-strength--fidelity Pareto frontier. The prototype-aware formulations account for mutual coupling, loss, discrete tunability, and static scattering. We evaluate the bounds on four experimental RIS-parametrized MIMO systems with up to 100 1-bit-programmable elements, using proxy-MNT models estimated from measurements. The resulting bounds are generally tight compared with feasible discrete-optimization outcomes. Interestingly, for coherency synthesis, the simpler bounds can in some cases be tighter than the fully prototype-aware bounds, highlighting their complementarity. Our results provide certified limits for wave-domain processing and harvesting of partially coherent microwaves.

</details>

# cond-mat.dis-nn [[Back]](#toc)

### [100] [Fractal Confinement and Magnetic Self-sabotage of Current Flow: Electrons Near the Metal-insulator Crossover in a 2D $δ$-layer](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.28739)
*Xinghai Zhang,Matthew S. Foster,Markus Mueller*

Main category: cond-mat.dis-nn

TL;DR: This paper studies low-temperature transport and itinerant magnetism in 2D δ-doped semiconductors, revealing two transport regimes near a critical density through large-scale Hartree-Fock calculations with random hoppings and repulsion. The conductivity initially increases as temperature drops below U0/10, but an insulating downturn occurs at T ~ U0/100 when a tiny density of magnetic moments forms, driven by fractal wavefunctions amplifying magnetic interactions and dynamic SU(2) symmetry breaking.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand the interplay between transport, disorder, and magnetism in 2D δ-doped semiconductor systems, particularly how itinerant magnetism emerges near a critical density and affects the metallic-to-insulating transition at low temperatures.

Method: Large-scale Hartree-Fock calculations incorporating random hoppings and on-site repulsion U0 to model 2D δ-doped semiconductors, tracking conductivity σ(T) as a function of temperature and examining magnetic moment formation and wavefunction fractal characteristics.

Result: Two distinct transport regimes are identified near a critical density: an initial metallic regime where σ(T) increases as T falls below U0/10, followed by a magnetic insulating regime near T ~ U0/100 where a small density of magnetic moments forms. Fractal wavefunctions locally amplify magnetic interactions, initially directing currents into branched filamentary paths, which are eventually suppressed by dynamic SU(2) symmetry breaking.

Conclusion: Magnetic moment formation and its coupling to fractal electronic wavefunctions, culminating in SU(2) symmetry breaking, drives a non-monotonic temperature dependence of conductivity in 2D δ-doped semiconductors, transitioning from metallic to insulating behavior at very low temperatures.

Abstract: We study low-$T$ transport and itinerant magnetism in 2D $δ$-doped semiconductors. Large-scale Hartree-Fock calculations with random hoppings and repulsion $U_0$ reveal two transport regimes near a critical density. The conductivity $σ(T)$ initially increases as $T$ drops below $U_0 / 10$. However, at $T \sim U_0/100$, a tiny density of magnetic moments forms, driving an insulating downturn. Fractal wavefunctions locally amplify magnetic interactions, initially concentrating currents into branched filaments, but ultimately choking them off via dynamic SU(2) symmetry breaking.

</details>


### [101] [Pseudo Entropy in Quantum Spin Chains: from Integrability to Chaos](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.29810)
*Tara Bahadur Rana,Yadav Raj Dahal,Kiran Adhikari*

Main category: cond-mat.dis-nn

TL;DR: The paper studies pseudo entropy and spectral dynamics in quantum spin chains (Heisenberg XXZ and mixed-field Ising models) as diagnostics of quantum chaos. It derives an exact relation between the real part of thermal pseudo entropy and the spectral form factor, showing characteristic dip, logarithmic ramp, and plateau signatures of chaos. The imaginary part provides information about Fisher zeros. Spatial pseudo entropy shows logarithmic critical scaling in both integrable and chaotic regimes, and non-positivity holds. An exact connection between pseudo entropy, relative pseudo entropy, and spectral complexity is established with potential holographic implications.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To determine whether pseudo entropy and spectral dynamics can serve as reliable diagnostics of quantum chaos in quantum many-body systems, and to explore their connections to spectral form factors, Fisher zeros, and holography.

Method: The authors study two quantum spin chain models (Heisenberg XXZ model and mixed-field Ising model), derive an exact analytical relation between the real part of thermal pseudo entropy and the spectral form factor, analyze the imaginary part for Fisher zero information, investigate spatial pseudo entropy scaling in both integrable and chaotic regimes, and establish exact connections between pseudo entropy, relative pseudo entropy, and spectral complexity.

Result: The real part of thermal pseudo entropy exhibits dip, logarithmic ramp, and plateau features characteristic of chaotic systems. The imaginary part provides complementary information about Fisher zeros. Spatial pseudo entropy maintains logarithmic critical scaling across integrable and chaotic regimes with non-positivity conjecture confirmed. Exact relations between pseudo entropy, relative pseudo entropy, and spectral complexity are derived with potential holographic connections.

Conclusion: Pseudo entropy can serve as a partial diagnostic of quantum chaos through its real part's connection to the spectral form factor, while its imaginary part offers unique information about Fisher zeros. The framework also bridges pseudo entropy to spectral complexity and potentially to holography.

Abstract: In this work, we study pseudo entropy and spectral dynamics in quantum spin chains (Heisenberg XXZ model and the mixed-field Ising model) to investigate whether they can serve as diagnostics of quantum chaos. First, we derive an exact relation between the real part of the thermal pseudo entropy and the spectral form factor. This provided evidence that pseudo entropy could exhibit the characteristic dip, logarithmic ramp, and plateau, implying the precise manner in which pseudo entropy probes chaos. We found that the imaginary part, while it does not diagnose chaos, can still provide crucial information about Fisher zeros that is not available in the real counterpart. For spatial pseudo entropy in the XXZ chain, we find that the logarithmic critical scaling persists in both integrable and chaotic regimes, and that the non-positivity conjecture holds. Finally, we derive an exact connection between pseudo entropy, relative pseudo entropy, and spectral complexity with a potential connection to holography.

</details>


### [102] [Hartree Fragmentation and Long-Range Pair Networks in Aperiodic Chains](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.30746)
*Yogeshwar Prasad*

Main category: cond-mat.dis-nn

TL;DR: This paper analyzes fermions in slowly varying aperiodic potentials with random long-range interactions V_ij/|i-j|^α. The potential fixes a resonance supply N_0 ∝ L^{2-n}/h. Crossing α-2n, Hartree fragmentation reconstructs the resonant-object ensemble: turning-point runs fragment into clusters without logarithmic singularity, while isolated wing bonds survive at finite density and restore a reduced logarithm. The scaling x_pair^{4-2n-α} ln{x_pair} ∝ h²/(V t0) is unchanged. Random coefficient fluctuations preserve the R^{-α} coupling, while uniform fluctuations cancel to R^{-α-2}.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand how slowly varying aperiodic potentials and random long-range interactions shape the resonant structure and coupling laws for fermions, particularly at the threshold α-2n where Hartree fragmentation alters the ensemble.

Method: Analytical study of fermionic systems in a slowly varying aperiodic potential with random interactions of power-law form V_ij/|i-j|^α, examining the resonance supply set by the potential, the crossing of α-2n, and Hartree fragmentation of turning-point runs into clusters. The analysis tracks isolated wing bonds, intrinsic matching laws, and the scaling behavior of pair corrections, along with the effect of random versus uniform coefficient fluctuations on the R^{-α} coupling.

Result: The resonance supply scales as N_0 ∝ L^{2-n}/h. At the α-2n crossing with Hartree fragmentation, turning-point runs fragment into clusters with no logarithmic singularity, while isolated wing bonds persist at finite density and restore a reduced logarithm. The pair scaling x_pair^{4-2n-α} ln{x_pair} ∝ h²/(V t0) remains invariant. Random fluctuations sustain the R^{-α} coupling; uniform fluctuations cancel to R^{-α-2}.

Conclusion: The interplay between aperiodic potentials and random long-range interactions yields a robust scaling law for pair corrections, while the structure of the resonant ensemble (clusters vs. wing bonds) is sensitive to the α-2n threshold and the nature of coefficient fluctuations.

Abstract: For fermions in a slowly varying aperiodic potential with random interactions $V_{ij}/|i-j|^α$, the potential fixes the resonance supply $N_0 \propto L^{2-n}/h$. On crossing $α-2n$, wherever Hartree fragmentation is operative, the resonant-object ensemble reconstructs: turning-point runs fragment into clusters whose intrinsic matching law resolves no logarithmic singularity, while isolated wing bonds survive at finite density and restore a reduced logarithm. The scaling $x^{4-2n-α}_{pair} ln{x_{pair}} \propto h^2/(V t0)$ is unchanged. Random coefficient fluctuations sustain the $R^{-α}$ coupling; uniform ones cancel to $R^{-α-2}$.

</details>


### [103] [Resonance statistics, Fock-space branching, and long-range pair networks in slowly varying interacting chains](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.30761)
*Yogeshwar Prasad*

Main category: cond-mat.dis-nn

TL;DR: This paper develops a microscopic resonance theory for a slowly varying aperiodic potential $h_i = h\cos(2\pi\beta i^n + \phi)$ with random power-law interactions $V_{ij}/|i-j|^\alpha$. It derives exact phase-averaged resonance statistics, constructs the resonant Fock-space graph, distinguishes one-step branching from connectivity, and identifies three distinct long-range thresholds ($\alpha=1/2$, $\alpha=2n$, $\alpha=2$) with different physical meanings—none being a localization transition by itself.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Building on a preceding letter that reported the resonant-object ensemble changing across $\alpha=2n$ under Hartree fragmentation, this work aims to provide the rigorous microscopic foundation for those results, including the exact resonance statistics, the domain of validity, and the distinction between pair-resonance scaling laws and shell-averaged bond laws.

Method: The authors derive exact phase-averaged resonance statistics (supply $N_0\propto L^{2-n}/h$, correlated common-phase comb, closed-form Hartree variance). They perform exact construction of the resonant Fock-space graph at $L\le18$ to study forward-branching and connectivity. They separate the fixed-pair matching law from the shell-averaged $q\ln(1/q)$ bond law, develop the comb into a mesoscopic shell theory, map the fragmentation domain with support threshold $V_*(\alpha)$ and boundary-healing recursion, and treat the marginal case $\alpha=2$ with double-logarithmic marginality.

Result: The leading supply law is proven unchanged by interactions. The order-one forward-branching scale $h_{\rm FB}\propto L^{2-n}$ supports no giant component, establishing inequivalence of one-step branching and connectivity. Three thresholds emerge with distinct meanings: $\alpha=1/2$ (exact variance threshold of random Fock-space energy, square-summability boundary of LIOM dressing), $\alpha=2n$ (change of local resonant objects), and $\alpha=2$ (marginality of the long-range shell sum).

Conclusion: The microscopic resonance theory is fully developed and validated, unifying the fragmentation picture, resonance statistics, and shell/matching laws under one framework. None of the three long-range thresholds by itself constitutes a localization transition.

Abstract: In a slowly varying aperiodic potential $h_i=h\cos(2πβi^n+φ)$ with random power-law density interactions $V_{ij}/|i-j|^α$, the resonant-object ensemble changes across $α=2n$, wherever Hartree fragmentation is operative, from isolated two-level bonds to a mixture of fragmented multi-site clusters and isolated wing bonds that survive at finite density, while the pair-resonance scaling $x_{\rm pair}^{4-2n-α}\ln x_{\rm pair}\propto h^2/(Vt_0)$ is unchanged~\cite{letter}. Here we develop the microscopic resonance theory underlying these results, together with its domain of validity. We derive the exact phase-averaged resonance statistics --- the supply $N_0\propto L^{2-n}/h$, the correlated common-phase comb, and the closed-form Hartree variance --- proving that the interaction leaves the leading supply law unchanged. Exact construction of the resonant Fock-space graph at $L\le18$ shows that the order-one forward-branching scale $h_{\rm FB}\propto L^{2-n}$ carries no giant component: one-step branching and connectivity are inequivalent. We separate the fixed-pair matching law from the shell-averaged $q\ln(1/q)$ law of the bond ensemble and develop the comb into a mesoscopic shell theory; we map the fragmentation domain, with its support threshold $V_*(α)$ and the boundary-healing recursion; and we treat the marginal case $α=2$, where shell and matching marginalities compound into a double logarithm. Three long-range thresholds emerge with distinct meanings --- $α=1/2$ (the exact variance threshold of the random Fock-space energy and the square-summability boundary of the leading LIOM-dressing estimate), $α=2n$ (change of the local resonant objects), and $α=2$ (marginality of the long-range shell sum) --- none of which is, by itself, a localization transition.

</details>


### [104] [Learning rules for complex-valued patterns in networks of oscillators](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.30800)
*Federico Sbravati,Aida Todri-Sanial*

Main category: cond-mat.dis-nn

TL;DR: This paper extends binary spin learning rules to complex-valued spins for representing grayscale patterns as multi-state neurons, proposes a rule outperforming Hebbian learning for pattern retrieval in oscillator networks, and proves an extension of the Diederich-Opper result for local and incremental learning.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To develop a natural and robust representation of grayscale patterns in associative memory networks, moving beyond binary spins to complex-valued multi-state neurons, while ensuring learning rules remain local and incremental—properties of interest for neural networks.

Method: The authors extend learning rules from real binary spins to complex-valued spins, storing patterns in a complex-valued weight matrix. They prove an extension of a result by Diederich and Opper for local/incremental learning within this complex-valued spin formulation, and test the associative memory behavior on systems of oscillators under various conditions.

Result: The proposed complex-valued learning rule performs better than standard methods (e.g., Hebbian learning) for encoding grayscale information suitable for pattern retrieval with oscillator networks. The extended Diederich-Opper result is proven, and associative memory performance is characterized for both real-valued and complex-valued correlated and random patterns.

Conclusion: Complex-valued spin formulations provide a robust and natural framework for grayscale pattern storage and retrieval in oscillator-based associative memories, with learning rules that are both superior to Hebbian learning and provably local and incremental.

Abstract: In this article, we extend learning rules from real binary to complex-valued spins. This formulation allows for a robust and natural representation of grayscale patterns, where spins behave as multi-state neurons and can be stored in a complex-valued weight matrix. We describe a rule that performs better than standard methods, such as Hebbian learning, to encode information in a suitable form for pattern retrieval with networks of oscillators. Since in neural networks it is of interest to have local and incremental learning rules, we prove the extension of a result by Diederich and Opper with our complex-valued spin formulation. We then test the behavior of the associative memory for the system of oscillators under different circumstances for both real-valued, as well as complex-valued correlated and random patterns.

</details>


<div id='cond-mat.str-el'></div>

# cond-mat.str-el [[Back]](#toc)

### [105] [Determinant Quantum-Quantum Monte Carlo: Coherent Auxiliary-Field Sampling](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.28742)
*Xuepeng Wang,Sagnik Banerjee,Debanjan Chowdhury*

Main category: cond-mat.str-el

TL;DR: DQ²MC is a quantum algorithm that lifts determinant quantum Monte Carlo (DQMC) onto a quantum computer, using a determinant oracle built from a block encoding of the single-particle action matrix via quantum singular value transformations. It requires only O(log N_st) system qubits without fermionic encodings, eliminates the Markov chain (full-quantum) or makes it rejection-free via exact heat-bath draws (hybrid), and maps the fermion sign problem onto quantum weak values, connecting exponential sign-problem cost with post-selection overhead.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Classical DQMC suffers from the fermion sign problem and Markov-chain autocorrelation times, and requires enumerating exponentially many Hubbard-Stratonovich weights. The authors aim to circumvent these bottlenecks by exploiting quantum computing capabilities to synthesize amplitudes directly and eliminate sampling inefficiencies.

Method: The algorithm constructs a determinant oracle that synthesizes DQMC amplitudes from a block encoding of the single-particle action matrix using quantum singular value transformations, avoiding explicit enumeration/precomputation of exponentially many Hubbard-Stratonovich weights. Two protocols are proposed: (1) a full-quantum protocol making observables interference amplitudes (eliminating Markov chains), and (2) a hybrid quantum-classical protocol with a constant-size active qubit block using exact heat-bath draws for rejection-free updates, supporting parallel tempering and distributed execution. The reweighting estimator behind the fermion sign problem is mapped onto quantum weak values.

Result: The circuit-depth scaling with spatial volume is more favorable than classical DQMC, at the cost of a post-selection overhead determined exactly by the largest target probability (polynomial for smooth distributions, exponential for sharply peaked ones). The fermion sign problem's exponential cost is shown to correspond precisely to the post-selection overhead of weak-value extraction.

Conclusion: DQ²MC provides a new quantum framework for many-fermion simulations that removes Markov-chain sampling bottlenecks and reframes the fermion sign problem as a quantum weak-value measurement overhead, opening a path toward quantum advantage in strongly correlated fermionic systems.

Abstract: We introduce determinant quantum-quantum Monte Carlo (DQ$^2$MC), a quantum algorithm that lifts the auxiliary-field sampling and averaging at the operational core of determinant quantum Monte Carlo onto a quantum computer. A determinant oracle synthesizes the DQMC amplitudes directly from a block encoding of the single-particle action matrix via quantum singular value transformations, so that the exponentially many Hubbard-Stratonovich weights are never enumerated, precomputed, or stored. Since the fermions are free for fixed auxiliary fields, the construction operates entirely at the single-particle level, requiring $O(\log N_{\mathrm{st}})$ system qubits and no Jordan-Wigner or Bravyi-Kitaev encoding, where $N_{\mathrm{st}}$ is the space-time volume. A full-quantum protocol makes observables interference amplitudes, eliminating the Markov chain and its autocorrelation time altogether; a hybrid quantum-classical protocol retains a constant-size active block of qubits and replaces the Metropolis-Hastings acceptance step with an exact heat-bath draw, so that cluster updates of any size are rejection-free, and passes only classical information between updates, admitting parallel tempering and distributed execution across quantum processors. The circuit-depth scales more favorably with spatial volume than classical DQMC, at the price of a post-selection overhead determined exactly by the largest target probability --- polynomial for smooth distributions, exponential for sharply peaked ones. Finally, the reweighting estimator underlying the fermion sign problem maps exactly onto a quantum weak value, placing the exponential cost of sign-problematic DQMC in precise correspondence with the post-selection overhead of weak-value extraction.

</details>


### [106] [Floquet engineering of competing antiferromagnetism and $d$-wave superconductivity on the square lattice](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.28750)
*Zhaoyu Han,Subir Sachdev*

Main category: cond-mat.str-el

TL;DR: The paper proposes a Floquet-driven Lieb-Hubbard quantum simulator that realizes a square-lattice spin-1/2 fermion model with independently tunable on-site repulsion and bond attraction, achieving a synthetic negative-U center despite microscopic repulsion, with mean-field results showing competing antiferromagnetic and d-wave superconducting phases.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To develop a quantum simulator capable of realizing fermionic models with independently tunable competing interactions (repulsive on-site and attractive bond), enabling access to intermediate-coupling regimes where these interactions are comparable to hopping, which is relevant for investigating the competition between magnetic and superconducting orders.

Method: The approach uses periodic modulation of the charge-transfer offset between d and p orbitals in a Lieb lattice to bring p-orbital doublons energetically close to d-manifold pairs while keeping p-orbital singlons off resonance, creating synthetic negative-U centers. Two controlled eliminations then generate compact bond attraction in the reduced d-only square-lattice model. Mean-field calculations are performed at half filling.

Result: The resulting bond interaction J is tunable independently of the Hubbard repulsion Ud, with photon-assisted path interference providing access to an intermediate-coupling regime where both J and Ud are comparable to hopping. Mean-field analysis at half filling finds adjacent antiferromagnetic and d-wave superconducting phases with narrow coexistence regions. The work also identifies a structural similarity between Floquet systems and electron-phonon problems.

Conclusion: The proposed driven Lieb-Hubbard simulator provides a realistic route to realize a square-lattice model with independently controlled repulsive and attractive interactions, and the identification of Floquet-electron-phonon structural similarities may guide future quantum-simulation protocol design.

Abstract: We propose a driven Lieb--Hubbard quantum simulator whose prethermal dynamics realize a square-lattice spin-1/2 fermion model with independently tunable repulsive on-site and attractive bond interactions. Periodically modulating the charge-transfer offset between the site ($d$) and bond ($p$) orbitals of the Lieb lattice brings a $p$-orbital doublon energetically close to a pair in the $d$ manifold while keeping all $p$-orbital singlons off resonance, thereby creating a synthetic ``negative-$U$'' center on a Lieb-lattice bond site. Two controlled eliminations then generate a compact bond attraction in the reduced $d$-only model on the square lattice, despite the microscopic repulsion in the $p$ orbital. The resulting interaction $J$ is tunable independently of the Hubbard repulsion $U_d$ on the $d$ orbitals, while interference between photon-assisted paths provides access to an intermediate-coupling regime in which $J$ and $U_d$ are both comparable to the effective hopping. At half filling, a mean-field calculation in this regime finds adjacent antiferromagnetic and $d$-wave superconducting phases, as well as narrow coexistence regions, suggesting close competition between these orders. We discuss the branch-preparation, prethermal, and higher-band conditions required to translate the formal construction into an optical-lattice protocol. More broadly, our work identifies a structural similarity between Floquet systems and electron-phonon problems that may guide the design of novel quantum-simulation protocols.

</details>


### [107] [Phase stability, charge ordering, and charge-liquid formation in the Falicov-Kimball model on triangular and kagome lattices](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.28898)
*Ammar Nejati,Marvin Lenk*

Main category: cond-mat.str-el

TL;DR: Using Markov-chain Monte Carlo simulations, the authors investigate the phase diagram of the spinless Falicov-Kimball model on triangular and kagome lattices under three filling conditions, with and without next-nearest-neighbor hopping. They find Anderson insulators, Mott insulators, and charge-density-wave phases on the triangular lattice, with evidence of a charge-liquid regime that shrinks from grand-canonical to conventional half-filling. On the kagome lattice, the CDW is absent and a Mott-like insulating ground state is found with possible quantum phase transition signatures.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To systematically determine how lattice geometry (non-bipartite vs. bipartite) and particle-hole symmetry breaking affect the phase diagram of the Falicov-Kimball model, which is a canonical model for correlated electron systems showing metal-insulator transitions, charge ordering, and exotic phases.

Method: Markov-chain Monte Carlo simulations of the spinless Falicov-Kimball model on triangular and kagome lattices. Three filling conditions are examined (fixed chemical potentials, generalized half-filling, and conventional half-filling), and perturbative next-nearest-neighbor hopping effects are studied for each.

Result: On the triangular lattice, Anderson insulator, Mott insulator, and CDW phases are found under all conditions, with CDW driven by Coulomb-interaction-mediated nesting. A charge-liquid regime is identified between weakly correlated and CDW regions, characterized by competing charge-ordering wavevectors and non-universal scaling, whose extent decreases from grand-canonical to conventional half-filling. On the kagome lattice, CDW is absent; instead a Mott-type insulating ground state is found with signatures of a possible quantum phase transition at zero temperature.

Conclusion: Lattice geometry and particle-hole symmetry breaking play a crucial role in shaping the phase diagram of correlated electron systems. The non-bipartite nature of the lattices leads to qualitatively different physics compared to bipartite lattices, with the kagome lattice showing complete suppression of charge ordering.

Abstract: We present a systematic investigation of the stability of the phase diagram of the spinless Falicov-Kimball model on two-dimensional non-bipartite lattices, the triangular and kagome lattices, using Markov-chain Monte Carlo simulations. Three filling or chemical potential conditions are examined: fixed chemical potentials ($μ_f = μ_c = U/2$), "generalized" half-filling ($\bar{n}_f = 1/3$, $\bar{n}_c = 2/3$), and conventional half-filling ($\bar{n}_f = \bar{n}_c = 1/2$). For each condition, the effects of a perturbatively small next-nearest-neighbor hopping are studied. On the triangular lattice, Anderson insulator, Mott insulator, and charge-density wave (CDW) phases are found in all cases, with the CDW driven by Coulomb-interaction-mediated nesting. Evidence for a charge-liquid regime is found in the region between the weakly correlated regime and the CDW, characterized by competing charge-ordering wavevectors and the absence of universal scaling behavior, whose extent shrinks progressively from the grand-canonical to the conventional half-filling condition. On the kagome lattice, the CDW phase is completely absent; instead, an insulating ground state, plausibly of the Mott type, is found, with signatures of a possible quantum phase transition at zero temperature. Our results establish the crucial role of lattice geometry and particle-hole symmetry breaking in shaping the phase diagram of correlated electron systems.

</details>


### [108] [Enhanced Diffusion from Twist-Angle Disorder](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.29062)
*Nicole S Ticea,Yi-Ming Wu*

Main category: cond-mat.str-el

TL;DR: This paper studies twist-angle disorder in twisted bilayer systems, modeling the twist angle as the curl of relative interlayer displacement (analogous to magnetic field from vector potential), and shows that geometric scattering constraints suppress momentum relaxation relative to white noise. The problem is mapped onto a non-linear sigma model (NLSM).


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Twisted bilayer systems are sensitive to tiny uncontrolled spatial variations in twist geometry, which can alter the parent Hamiltonian and affect coarse-grained properties like transport and ordering. Understanding how twist-angle disorder impacts single-particle properties is therefore crucial.

Method: The twist angle is modeled as the curl of the relative displacement between the two bilayers, drawing an analogy to electrodynamics (magnetic field = curl of vector potential). The resulting disorder potential obeys geometric scattering constraints, and the problem is formulated within a non-linear sigma model (NLSM) framework.

Result: Geometric scattering constraints imposed by the twist structure substantially suppress momentum relaxation relative to mean-rate-matched white-noise disorder, and the effects are quantified through the NLSM mapping.

Conclusion: Twist-angle disorder in twisted bilayer systems is qualitatively different from conventional white-noise disorder due to intrinsic geometric constraints, leading to suppressed momentum relaxation and requiring a non-linear sigma model treatment for accurate description.

Abstract: We consider the effect of twist-angle disorder on the single-particle properties of twisted bilayer systems. These materials are a natural playground for studying how tiny, uncontrolled spatial variations may become unusually consequential; a local change in the twist geometry can give rise to spatially-correlated alterations of the parent Hamiltonian, which in turn affect the coarse-grained properties of the substrate such as transport and ordering tendencies. In analogy to electrodynamics, where the magnetic field is given by the curl of the vector potential, we model the twist angle as the curl of the relative displacement between the two bilayers. We find that geometric scattering constraints imposed here by the twist structure substantially suppress momentum relaxation relative to mean-rate-matched white noise. We quantify these effects and show that the problem can be mapped onto a non-linear sigma model (NLSM).

</details>


### [109] [Duality Between Twist-Angle Disorder and Non-Hermitian Disorder](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.29064)
*Yi-Ming Wu,Nicole S. Ticea*

Main category: cond-mat.str-el

TL;DR: The paper proposes a model of moiré heterobilayer systems with twist-angle disorder, where the twist angle is uniform only within finite domains and randomly deforms at larger scales. Using the replica trick, the authors show that disorder averaging produces alternating-sign correlations between replicas. They establish a duality between the low-energy moiré bands and a model with non-Hermitian disorder that can evade Anderson localization, offering a new perspective on twist-angle disorder in moiré systems.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand how twist-angle disorder (as opposed to conventional disorder) affects moiré heterobilayer systems, particularly regarding correlation structure and localization properties.

Method: The authors use the replica trick to perform disorder averaging over random local twist-angle fluctuations, and establish a duality mapping between low-energy moiré bands with twist-angle disorder and a fermionic model coupled to non-Hermitian disorder.

Result: Disorder averaging produces nontrivial alternating-sign correlations between replicas, maximized near local domain boundaries and along certain directions. The dual non-Hermitian disorder model can dynamically evade Anderson localization, revealing essential differences between twist-angle disorder and conventional disorder.

Conclusion: The proposed duality provides a new framework to study twist-angle disorder in moiré systems and highlights fundamental distinctions between this type of disorder and conventional disorder, particularly in terms of localization behavior.

Abstract: We propose and study a model of moire heterobilayer systems where the twist angle remains uniform and well defined only within a finite range, but deforms randomly at larger distances. The local twist angle is then subject to a certain probability distribution that penalizes large fluctuations around its mean value. Within the framework of the replica trick, we show that disorder averaging produces a nontrivial correlation with alternating sign between replicas, maximized only close the boundary of each local domain and along certain directions. We show that for the low-energy moire bands, exactly the same correlation can be generated in a dual model where fermions are coupled to non-Hermitian disorder, which can evade Anderson localization dynamically. This duality provides a new perspective to investigate twist-angle disorder in moire systems, and unveils some of the essential differences between twist-angle disorder and conventional disorder.

</details>


### [110] [Real-space manifestation of ferroic multipoles in altermagnetic MnF$_2$](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.29089)
*Iurii Kibalin,Dalila Bounoua,José A. Rodriguez Velamazán,Oscar Fabelo,Navid Qureshi,Quentin Faure,Philippe Bourges,Victor Balédent,Jian-Rui Soh,Jeffrey Rau,Paul McClarty,Arsen Gukasov*

Main category: cond-mat.str-el

TL;DR: Using polarized-neutron diffraction, researchers reconstructed the 3D magnetization density of the altermagnet MnF₂, directly observing the spin density that reveals anisotropic Mn magnetization consistent with the symmetry-allowed altermagnetic rank-5 multipole O₅₂ (magnetic triacontadipole), providing the first real-space evidence of ferroic multipolar order in an altermagnet.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Altermagnets host a magnetic multipolar order parameter, yet direct real-space observation of these multipoles has remained elusive. The authors aim to provide direct experimental evidence of multipolar order in an altermagnet.

Method: The authors employed polarized-neutron diffraction to reconstruct the three-dimensional magnetization density of MnF₂. They exploited symmetry-selective magnetic reflections to separate contributions from the dominant spherical Mn²⁺ density, the weaker anisotropic Mn magnetization, and the covalent spin polarization of fluorine ligands.

Result: The reconstructed spin density reveals a finite fluorine ion moment and an anisotropic Mn magnetization consistent with the symmetry-allowed altermagnetic rank-5 magnetic multipole O₅₂ (magnetic triacontadipole), providing direct real-space evidence of ferroic multipolar order in an altermagnet.

Conclusion: Polarized-neutron diffraction is established as a powerful probe for visualizing hidden magnetic multipoles in quantum materials, enabling direct real-space observation of multipolar order in altermagnets.

Abstract: Altermagnets are unconventional spin split magnets arising from the zero spin-orbit coupled limit. They host a magnetic multipolar order parameter yet direct real-space observation of these multipoles has remained elusive. Here we use polarized-neutron diffraction to reconstruct the three-dimensional magnetization density of the prototypical altermagnet MnF$_2$. By exploiting symmetry-selective magnetic reflections, we separate the dominant spherical Mn$^{2+}$ contribution from the much weaker anisotropic Mn magnetization and the covalent spin polarization of the fluorine ligands. The reconstructed spin density reveals a finite fluorine ion moment together with an anisotropic Mn magnetization consistent with the symmetry-allowed altermagnetic rank-5 magnetic multipole $O_{52}$(magnetic triacontadipole). These results provide direct real-space evidence of ferroic multipolar order in an altermagnet and establish polarized-neutron diffraction as a powerful probe of hidden magnetic multipoles in quantum materials.

</details>


### [111] [Electronic phase separation and emergence of a nondimerized insulating phase in VO$_2$ $(110)_{\mathit{R}}$ ultrathin films](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.29116)
*S. Inoue,D. Shiga,R. Hayasaka,K. Ozawa,A. F. Santander-Syro,H. Kumigashira*

Main category: cond-mat.str-el

TL;DR: This study uses in situ photoemission and X-ray absorption spectroscopy to investigate thickness-dependent electronic structure and V-V dimerization in VO2/TiO2(110) ultrathin films. Unlike VO2(001)R films, geometric constraints on dimerization are absent in the (110)R orientation, yet a nondimerized insulating phase still emerges at 1 nm, attributed to enhanced Mott instability from reduced dimensionality. However, the nondimerized phase coexists with the metallic phase and grows exponentially with decreasing thickness, yielding an effective critical thickness of ~2.2 nm, implying that V-V chain orientation dictates phase separation via strain-mediated competition.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To determine whether the nondimerized insulating phase in VO2 ultrathin films arises from a universal Mott instability enhanced by reduced dimensionality or from geometric constraints on V-V dimerization, by studying the (110)R orientation where no such geometric constraint exists.

Method: In situ photoemission spectroscopy and X-ray absorption spectroscopy were performed on VO2/TiO2(110) ultrathin films of varying thickness, examining the temperature-driven metal-insulator transition and V-V dimerization as a function of film thickness.

Result: The metal-insulator transition persists down to 1.5 nm, but at 1 nm a nondimerized insulating phase emerges. Unlike the (001)R case, this nondimerized phase coexists with the dimerized metal-insulator-transition phase; its fraction increases exponentially with decreasing thickness and dominates at 1 nm, with an effective critical thickness of approximately 2.2 nm.

Conclusion: The emergence of the nondimerized insulating phase in VO2 ultrathin films is driven by a common enhancement of Mott instability due to reduced dimensionality, independent of geometric constraints. However, the in-plane orientation of V-V chains controls the spatial evolution of electronic phase separation through strain-mediated phase competition.

Abstract: Using in situ photoemission spectroscopy and x-ray absorption spectroscopy, we investigated the thickness dependence of the electronic structure and V-V dimerization in VO$_2$/TiO$_2$ (110) ultrathin films, in which the one-dimensional V-V chains along the $c_{\mathit{R}}$ axis lie in the film plane. In VO$_2$ $(110)_{\mathit{R}}$ films, the reduction in dimensionality along the surface-normal direction is not expected to impose a geometric constraint on V-V dimerization, unlike in VO$_2$ $(001)_{\mathit{R}}$ films. Nevertheless, the characteristic spectral changes associated with the temperature-driven metal-insulator transition observed in thick films persist down to 1.5 nm, whereas at 1 nm an insulating electronic phase is observed without V-V dimerization. This behavior is highly similar to that reported for VO$_2$ $(001)_{\mathit{R}}$, suggesting that the enhancement of Mott instability resulting from reduced dimensionality is a common and essential driving force for the emergence of the nondimerized insulating phase in VO$_2$ ultrathin films. Meanwhile, unlike in VO$_2$ $(001)_{\mathit{R}}$, the nondimerized insulating phase in VO$_2$ $(110)_{\mathit{R}}$ coexists with the phase exhibiting the temperature-driven metal-insulator transition. Its fraction increases exponentially with decreasing thickness and becomes dominant at 1 nm. The corresponding effective critical thickness is estimated to be 2.2 nm. These results imply that the geometric orientation of the V-V chains dictates the spatial evolution of electronic phase separation via strain-mediated phase competition.

</details>


### [112] [Evolving charge order in the CDW state of AV$_3$Sb$_5$ metals](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.29142)
*Anshu Kataria,Francis Pratt,Peter J. Baker,Stephen Cottrell,Miki Bonacci,Andrea Capa Salinas,Stephen D. Wilson,Zurab Guguchia,Samuele Sanna,Pietro Bonfà*

Main category: cond-mat.str-el

TL;DR: This paper extends muon-spin spectroscopy studies of AV₃Sb₅ kagome metals (A = Cs, K) using avoided level crossing (ALC) measurements, identifying a second electronic transition within the charge density wave phase that involves an additional charge modulation mechanism beyond internal magnetic fields.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: The electronic state preceding the superconducting phase in AV₃Sb₅ kagome metals remains poorly understood, despite extensive studies of their intertwined electronic and structural orders.

Method: Extended avoided level crossing (ALC) muon-spin spectroscopy from a previous RbV₃Sb₅ study [Phys. Rev. Research 7, L032046 (2025)] to CsV₃Sb₅ and KV₃Sb₅ systems.

Result: Consistent with prior work on RbV₃Sb₅, a second transition is identified in Cs and K systems that cannot be explained solely by internal magnetic fields, indicating an additional electronic mechanism subtly modifying charge distribution within the V plane. The ALC results indicate additional charge modulation within the CDW phase occurring at T* < T_CDW for Cs and Rb, or near T_CDW for K.

Conclusion: An additional charge modulation exists within the CDW phase of AV₃Sb₅ kagome metals, with its onset temperature dependent on the alkali metal A, suggesting a composition-dependent electronic mechanism beyond conventional magnetic origins.

Abstract: AV$_3$Sb$_5$ kagome metals are characterized by intertwined electronic and structural orders, which motivated extensive studies in recent years. Yet the details of the electronic state preceding the superconducting phase remain poorly understood. Here we extend our previous investigation [Phys. Rev. Research 7, L032046 (2025)] of RbV$_3$Sb$_5$ using avoided level crossing (ALC) muon-spin spectroscopy to the $A$ = Cs and K systems. Consistent with our previous study, we identify a second transition whose origin cannot be attributed solely to an internal magnetic field, indicating the involvement of an additional electronic mechanism that subtly modifies the charge distribution within the V plane. In particular, the ALC results point towards an additional charge modulation taking place within the charge density wave (CDW) phase and occurring at $T^{*}<T_{CDW}$ for $A$ = Cs and Rb, or in the vicinity of $T_{CDW}$ for $A$ = K.

</details>


### [113] [Crystal electric field excitations and an effective-spin-$1/2$ ground doublet in the hyperkagome magnet Yb$_3$Sc$_2$Ga$_3$O$_{12}$](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.29276)
*Tingjun Zhang,Zehao Wang,Douglas L. Abernathy,Rong-Zhu Lin,Steven J. Gomez Alvarado,Chien-Lung Huang,Pengcheng Dai*

Main category: cond-mat.str-el

TL;DR: Using inelastic neutron scattering, the authors determine the crystal electric field excitations of Yb3+ in the hyperkagome magnet Yb3Sc2Ga3O12, revealing three nearly dispersionless transitions near 58, 68, and 74 meV from the ground-state Kramers doublet. A Stevens-operator analysis constrained by D2 symmetry reproduces the spectrum and yields an Ising-type g tensor, establishing a well-isolated Jeff=1/2 degree of freedom. The measured CEF scheme revises earlier bulk-inferred levels while preserving the low-energy pseudospin picture, and the resulting single-ion model reproduces susceptibility, magnetization, and Schottky specific heat.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To establish the microscopic single-ion crystal electric field basis for Yb3Sc2Ga3O12, a rare-earth hyperkagome magnet, which is essential for constructing an effective exchange Hamiltonian and interpreting low-energy collective excitations in this frustrated quantum magnet.

Method: Inelastic neutron scattering to directly measure CEF excitations; Stevens-operator Hamiltonian analysis constrained by local D2 (222) symmetry; two independent fitting protocols to extract excitation energies, ground-doublet wave functions, and g tensors; comparison of the single-ion model to bulk susceptibility, magnetization, and specific heat.

Result: Three nearly dispersionless magnetic excitations at ~58, 68, and 74 meV; an Ising-type ground-state g tensor; the first excited doublet at ~58 meV above ground, giving a well-isolated Jeff=1/2 degree of freedom. The directly measured CEF spectrum revises the earlier bulk-inferred level scheme but preserves the essential low-energy pseudospin description. Two fitting protocols yield consistent excitation energies, wave functions, and g tensors despite nonunique CEF parameters. The model reproduces susceptibility, magnetization, and the field evolution of the Schottky anomaly.

Conclusion: The study establishes a reliable microscopic single-ion CEF model for Yb3+ in Yb3Sc2Ga3O12, providing the essential basis for constructing an effective exchange Hamiltonian and for interpreting future low-energy collective excitation measurements in this hyperkagome quantum spin liquid candidate.

Abstract: We use inelastic neutron scattering (INS) to determine the crystal electric field (CEF) excitations of Yb$^{3+}$ in the rare-earth hyperkagome magnet Yb$_3$Sc$_2$Ga$_3$O$_{12}$. Three nearly dispersionless magnetic excitations are observed near 58, 68, and 74~meV, corresponding to transitions from the ground-state Kramers doublet to the three excited doublets of the $J=7/2$ multiplet. A Stevens-operator analysis constrained by the local $222$ ($D_2$) symmetry reproduces the excitation energies and spectral weights and yields an Ising-type ground-state $g$ tensor. The first excited doublet lies approximately 58~meV above the ground state, establishing a well-isolated effective $J_{\mathrm{eff}}=1/2$ degree of freedom over the low-temperature regime relevant to collective magnetism. Notably, the directly measured CEF spectrum substantially revises the level scheme previously inferred from bulk measurements, while preserving the essential low-energy pseudospin description. Two independent fitting protocols give consistent excitation energies, ground-doublet wave functions, and $g$ tensors, despite the nonuniqueness of the individual CEF parameters. The resulting single-ion model also reproduces the characteristic susceptibility, magnetization, and field evolution of the Schottky anomaly in specific heat. These results establish the microscopic single-ion basis needed to construct an effective exchange Hamiltonian and to interpret future measurements of low-energy collective excitations in Yb$_3$Sc$_2$Ga$_3$O$_{12}$.

</details>


### [114] [Experimental observation of decoupled spin subsystems in decorated square kagomé lattice magnets of the nabokoite family](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.29323)
*V. N. Glazkov,Ya V. Rebrov,M. A. Dubovitskii,M. M. Markina,A. F. Murtazoev,P. S. Berdonosov,A. N. Vasiliev*

Main category: cond-mat.str-el

TL;DR: Nabokoite-family compounds, which extend square kagomé lattice physics into 3D via interlayer spins, exhibit two virtually decoupled spin subsystems. Absolute-calibrated EPR reveals only a fraction of copper spins are EPR-active and order at the Néel point, while the EPR-silent subsystem's susceptibility contribution decreases on cooling—demonstrating coexisting magnetic order and possible spin-liquid dynamics in a single compound.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To challenge conventional models of unified exchange networks in decorated frustrated lattices by investigating whether the complex 3D exchange network in nabokoite compounds can be decomposed into independent spin subsystems, and to directly observe coexistence of magnetic order and spin-liquid-like behavior within a single material.
